<skill_content name="servicenow-mcp-reference">
# Skill: servicenow-mcp-reference

# ServiceNow MCP Tool Reference

Canonical catalog of `servicenow` MCP tools and artifact deployment rules. Use this as the single source of truth for the `servicenow` agent.

The MCP implementation lives at `/Users/lasn/Projects/servicenow-platform-mcp`. When tool behavior is unclear, inspect that implementation before guessing. Prefer the implementation over memory, stale prompt text, or assumptions.

## Source of Truth and Safety Model

Use this priority order when deciding how to call the MCP:

1. The live tool schema exposed in the current session.
2. The implementation at `/Users/lasn/Projects/servicenow-platform-mcp`.
3. This skill.
4. Agent prompt summaries.

Never invent tool names, parameters, artifact types, table names, or field names. If the current tool surface differs from this skill, follow the live tool schema and treat this file as guidance only.

## Supported Artifact Types (17)

`artifact_create` and `artifact_update` support the following 17 artifact types, each mapped to its underlying ServiceNow table automatically (e.g., `business_rule` -> `sys_script`, `script_include` -> `sys_script_include`):

`business_rule`, `script_include`, `client_script`, `ui_policy`, `ui_action`, `fix_script`, `scheduled_job`, `scripted_rest_resource`, `ui_script`, `processor`, `widget`, `ui_page`, `ui_macro`, `script_action`, `mid_script_include`, `scripted_rest_api`, `notification_script`

## Artifact Write Tools

### `artifact_create`

Creates a new platform artifact. Accepts:

- `artifact_type` -- one of the 17 supported types
- `data` -- JSON string of field values (all values must be strings: `"true"` not `true`, `"1"` not `1`)
- `script_path` (optional) -- absolute path to a local file containing the script body; must be UTF-8, under `SCRIPT_ALLOWED_ROOT` if configured, max 1MB

Automatically maps the script field for the artifact type (e.g., `operation_script` for Scripted REST Resources, `client_script` for widgets). Returns the `sys_id` of the created record.

### `artifact_update`

Updates an existing artifact by `sys_id`. Accepts:

- `artifact_type` -- one of the 17 supported types
- `sys_id` -- target record
- `changes` -- JSON string of fields to update
- `script_path` (optional) -- same semantics as `artifact_create`

### Hard Rule

**Do NOT use `record_create`, `record_update`, `record_preview_create`, or `record_preview_update` on script artifact tables.** Always use `artifact_create` / `artifact_update` for any of the 17 types listed above. The artifact tools validate types, handle script field mapping, and enforce path security; the record tools do none of this.

`record_create` / `record_update` remain appropriate for data records (incidents, changes, CMDB CIs, custom tables) that are NOT script artifacts.

### JSON Escaping Rules

- All field values must be strings
- Always include the full script body, never truncate
- Escape newlines as `\\n` and single quotes inside scripts as `\\'` when embedding in the JSON `data` string
- **No em-dashes** in scripts - ServiceNow may corrupt them
- **Use `script_path`** when the script is available as a local file -- avoids JSON escaping issues and keeps scripts readable

## Pre-Development Checklist

Before creating or modifying any artifact on a table, run these tools to build context and avoid conflicts:

1. **`docs_logic_map(table="<target_table>")`** -- List ALL existing automations on the table (Business Rules, Client Scripts, UI Policies, etc. grouped by lifecycle phase). Prevents creating conflicting or redundant logic.
2. **`meta_what_writes(table="<target>", field="<field>")`** -- Find business rules and other automated mechanisms that write to a specific table (and optionally a specific field). Run before adding new logic that updates a field. Skip when no specific field is in scope.
3. **`meta_find_references(target="<name>")`** -- If refactoring an existing Script Include or table, find what references it before changing behavior.
4. **`table_describe(table="<target>")`** / **`meta_list_artifacts(artifact_type="<type>")`** -- Understand the schema (field names, types, references, choices) or discover existing artifacts of a type. Use `meta_get_artifact` to fetch a specific artifact's full script body.

Run applicable read-only tools in parallel. Do not parallelize writes. Skip steps that don't apply.

## Safe Operating Workflows

### Query Safety

Always use `build_query` before `table_query` or `table_aggregate`. Pass the returned `query_token`; do not hand-write encoded query strings unless the live tool schema explicitly requires them.

For large or noisy tables such as `syslog`, `sys_audit`, email logs, import sets, and transaction tables, always include a time-bound condition first. If a query is blocked by large table protection, narrow the time range and retry once.

Default to small result sets. Ask before pulling broad lists when the user's goal can be answered with an aggregate, a targeted lookup, or a narrower query.

### Data Record Write Safety

For data records, preview before apply:

1. Use `record_preview_create`, `record_preview_update`, or `record_preview_delete`.
2. Show the user the important changes and risks.
3. Use `record_apply` only after explicit confirmation.

Data records include incidents, changes, problems, requests, knowledge articles, CMDB CIs, and custom table records that are not script artifacts.

Do not skip preview unless the user explicitly asks for a direct write and the operation is non-destructive.

### Script Artifact Write Safety

For script artifacts, never use the record preview or record CRUD tools. Use `artifact_create` and `artifact_update` only.

Before modifying an existing artifact:

1. Fetch the current artifact with `meta_get_artifact`.
2. Check references with `docs_artifact_summary` or `meta_find_references` when behavior changes could affect callers.
3. Deploy with `artifact_update`.
4. Run `docs_review_notes`.
5. Run `docs_test_scenarios`.
6. Report the `sys_id`, what changed, review findings, and suggested tests.

### Destructive and Risky Operations

Require explicit user confirmation naming the target before:

- Deleting records
- Running fix scripts
- Deactivating or reactivating automations
- Changing system properties
- Updating production data
- Updating ACLs, roles, groups, or access-related records
- Making broad updates that affect more than one record
- Changing integration endpoints, credentials, MID server behavior, or scheduled jobs

When in doubt, stop and ask for confirmation. Explain the likely blast radius in one or two sentences.

### Production Guardrails

Respect MCP write gating. If production writes are blocked, do not work around the guardrail. Provide the prepared change, validation steps, and ask the user to run it through the approved release path.

Never log, print, or copy masked secrets. Treat values returned as `***MASKED***` as intentionally unavailable.

## MCP Tool Catalog

### Introspection

- **`table_describe`** -- Field metadata for a table: types, references, choices, attributes. Run before writing any script that targets a table.
- **`table_query`** -- Query any table using an encoded query. Always call `build_query` first to produce the `query_token`.
- **`record_get`** -- Fetch a single record by sys_id from any table.
- **`table_aggregate`** -- Count, avg, min, max, sum with optional `group_by`. Requires `query_token` from `build_query`.

### Metadata (Platform Artifacts)

- **`meta_list_artifacts`** -- List artifacts by type: `business_rule`, `script_include`, `ui_policy`, `ui_action`, `client_script`, `scheduled_job`, `fix_script`, etc. Use to discover existing artifacts and avoid duplication.
- **`meta_get_artifact`** -- Fetch full artifact details including the script body. Use before modifying any existing artifact.

### Change Intelligence

- **`meta_find_references`** -- Search all scripts on the instance for a target string (script include name, table name, property key). Use before refactoring or renaming.
- **`meta_what_writes`** -- Find business rules and other automated mechanisms that write to a specific table (and optionally a specific field). Run before adding new logic that updates a field.

### Documentation

- **`docs_logic_map`** -- Lifecycle map of ALL automations on a table (before/after insert/update, display, async). Grouped by lifecycle phase. Run before adding any new automation to a table.
- **`docs_artifact_summary`** -- Summary of an artifact with dependency analysis (what it touches, what touches it). Use before modifying an artifact to understand blast radius.
- **`docs_review_notes`** -- Anti-pattern scan for a specific artifact: GlideRecord in loops, non-portable sys_ids, unbounded queries, and similar smells. Run after writing or modifying a script. A hardcoded sys_id is valid when the referenced record keeps that identifier across target instances, such as when it moves through the same update set.
- **`docs_test_scenarios`** -- Suggested test scenarios derived from script analysis. Run after writing a script to present test coverage to the user.

### Query

- **`build_query`** -- Convert a JSON array of conditions into an encoded `query_token`. MUST be called before `table_query` or `table_aggregate`. Never pass raw encoded query strings directly.

  Each condition object has `operator`, `field`, and `value`. Operators include `equals`, `not_equals`, `contains`, `starts_with`, `greater_than`, `less_than`, `days_ago`, `is_empty`, `between`, `in_list`, `order_by`.

### Domain Tools

Use domain tools for common ITSM and CMDB reads because they are simpler and purpose-built. For domain writes, still follow the data record write safety rules unless the user explicitly requested a direct non-destructive write.

#### Incidents

- **`incident_list`** -- List incidents with filters such as state, priority, assignee, or assignment group.
- **`incident_get`** -- Fetch an incident by number, for example `INC0010042`.
- **`incident_create`** -- Create an incident. Requires a short description.
- **`incident_update`** -- Update an incident by number.
- **`incident_resolve`** -- Resolve an incident with close code and close notes.
- **`incident_add_comment`** -- Add a comment or work note to an incident.

#### Changes

- **`change_list`** -- List change requests with filters such as state, type, or risk.
- **`change_get`** -- Fetch a change request by number.
- **`change_create`** -- Create a change request.
- **`change_update`** -- Update a change request by number.
- **`change_tasks`** -- List tasks for a change request.
- **`change_add_comment`** -- Add a comment or work note to a change request.

#### Problems

- **`problem_list`** -- List problem records with filters.
- **`problem_get`** -- Fetch a problem by number.
- **`problem_create`** -- Create a problem record.
- **`problem_update`** -- Update a problem by number.
- **`problem_root_cause`** -- Document root cause analysis on a problem.

#### Requests

- **`request_list`** -- List request records with filters.
- **`request_get`** -- Fetch a request by number.
- **`request_items`** -- List RITMs for a request.
- **`request_item_get`** -- Fetch a RITM by number.
- **`request_item_update`** -- Update a RITM.

#### Knowledge

- **`knowledge_search`** -- Search knowledge articles by text.
- **`knowledge_get`** -- Fetch a knowledge article by number or sys_id.
- **`knowledge_create`** -- Create a knowledge article.
- **`knowledge_update`** -- Update a knowledge article.
- **`knowledge_feedback`** -- Submit rating or feedback on an article.

#### Service Catalog

- **`sc_catalogs_list`** -- List service catalogs the user has access to.
- **`sc_catalog_get`** -- Fetch details of a specific service catalog.
- **`sc_categories_list`** -- List categories for a specific service catalog.
- **`sc_category_get`** -- Fetch details of a specific service catalog category.
- **`sc_items_list`** -- List catalog items with optional filters (catalog, category, text).
- **`sc_item_get`** -- Fetch details of a specific catalog item.
- **`sc_item_variables`** -- Fetch variables (form fields) for a specific catalog item.
- **`sc_order_now`** -- Order a catalog item immediately, bypassing the cart.
- **`sc_add_to_cart`** -- Add a catalog item to the shopping cart.
- **`sc_cart_get`** -- Retrieve the current user's shopping cart contents.
- **`sc_cart_submit`** -- Submit the current shopping cart as an order request.
- **`sc_cart_checkout`** -- Checkout the current shopping cart (two-step ordering).

#### CMDB

- **`cmdb_list`** -- List CIs with optional class and operational status filters.
- **`cmdb_get`** -- Fetch a CI by name or sys_id.
- **`cmdb_relationships`** -- Get parent and child relationships for a CI.
- **`cmdb_classes`** -- List known CI classes.
- **`cmdb_health`** -- Aggregate CMDB operational health.

### Record CRUD

Use record tools for data records only. Do not use them for script artifact tables.

- **`record_get`** -- Fetch a single record by sys_id from any table.
- **`record_preview_create`** -- Preview a data record creation and return a preview token.
- **`record_preview_update`** -- Preview a data record update and return a preview token plus diff.
- **`record_preview_delete`** -- Preview a data record delete and return a preview token.
- **`record_apply`** -- Apply a previously previewed record operation after explicit user confirmation.
- **`record_create`** -- Directly create a data record. Use only when preview is not required or the user explicitly asked for a direct non-destructive write.
- **`record_update`** -- Directly update a data record. Use only when preview is not required or the user explicitly asked for a direct non-destructive write.
- **`record_delete`** -- Directly delete a data record. Requires explicit confirmation naming the table and record.

### Artifact Write

- **`artifact_create`** -- Create a new platform artifact (any of the 17 types). See "Artifact Write Tools" above for full semantics. Preferred over `record_create` for script artifacts.
- **`artifact_update`** -- Update an existing platform artifact by sys_id. See "Artifact Write Tools" above. Preferred over `record_update` for script artifacts.

### Attachment (Read)

- **`attachment_list`** -- List attachment metadata records with optional filters (table, sys_id, file_name).
- **`attachment_get`** -- Fetch attachment metadata by attachment sys_id.
- **`attachment_download`** -- Download attachment content by attachment sys_id.
- **`attachment_download_by_name`** -- Download attachment content by source record and file name.

### Attachment (Write)

- **`attachment_upload`** -- Upload a new attachment using base64-encoded content.
- **`attachment_delete`** -- Delete an attachment by attachment sys_id.

### Relationships

- **`rel_references_to`** -- Find records that reference a target record.
- **`rel_references_from`** -- Find records referenced by a source record.

### Change Intelligence

- **`changes_updateset_inspect`** -- Inspect update set members grouped by type, with risk flags where available.
- **`changes_diff_artifact`** -- Produce a unified diff between recent versions of an artifact.
- **`changes_last_touched`** -- Show who last changed a record and what changed.
- **`changes_release_notes`** -- Generate release notes from an update set (Markdown format via the `format` parameter).

### Debug and Trace

- **`debug_trace`** -- Build a merged timeline from audit, journal, and log data for a record.
- **`debug_flow_execution`** -- Inspect Flow Designer execution details.
- **`debug_email_trace`** -- Reconstruct email activity for a record.
- **`debug_integration_health`** -- Inspect recent integration errors from queues or REST messages.
- **`debug_importset_run`** -- Inspect import set header, row results, and errors.
- **`debug_field_mutation_story`** -- Show chronological mutation history for a single field.

### Flow Designer

- **`flow_list`** -- List Flow Designer flows and subflows with optional filters (table, flow_type, status, active_only).
- **`flow_get`** -- Fetch a Flow Designer flow definition including its input and output variables.
- **`flow_map`** -- Map the structure of a flow: action instances and logic blocks in order.
- **`flow_action_detail`** -- Fetch detailed info about a flow action instance, its type definition, and configured steps.
- **`flow_execution_list`** -- List Flow Designer execution contexts with optional filters (flow, source record, state).
- **`flow_execution_detail`** -- Fetch detailed execution info including ordered log entries for a Flow Designer context.
- **`flow_snapshot_list`** -- List published snapshots (versions) for a Flow Designer flow.
- **`workflow_migration_analysis`** -- Analyze a legacy workflow version for Flow Designer migration readiness.

### Workflow (Legacy)

- **`workflow_contexts`** -- List legacy workflow and Flow Designer contexts running on a record.
- **`workflow_map`** -- Show the structure of a workflow version: activities, transitions, activity variables.
- **`workflow_status`** -- Show execution status of a workflow context: currently executing and completed steps.
- **`workflow_activity_detail`** -- Fetch detailed info about a workflow activity, its element definition, and configured variables.
- **`workflow_version_list`** -- List workflow versions defined for a specific table.

### Investigations

Investigations are read-oriented analysis tools. Run the investigation first, then explain individual findings when needed.

- **`investigate_run`** -- Run an investigation such as `stale_automations`, `deprecated_apis`, `table_health`, `acl_conflicts`, `error_analysis`, `slow_transactions`, or `performance_bottlenecks`.
- **`investigate_explain`** -- Explain a specific finding from an investigation result.

### Utility

- **`list_tool_packages`** -- List available ServiceNow MCP tool packages.

## Implementation Reference

The local MCP source is available at `/Users/lasn/Projects/servicenow-platform-mcp`.

Use it to verify:

- Exact tool names and parameters
- Supported artifact type mappings
- Script field mapping by artifact type
- Deny-listed tables and protected fields
- Row limits and large table protections
- Production write gates
- Preview token and apply behavior
- Path restrictions for `script_path`
- Validation behavior for `artifact_create` and `artifact_update`

When hardening agents, keep operational behavior in the agent files and detailed MCP mechanics in this skill. This avoids drift between `servicenow` and the shared reference.

## Verification Checklist for MCP Work

Before reporting ServiceNow MCP work as complete:

1. Required read-only context was gathered before writes.
2. Queries used `build_query` and bounded large tables by time.
3. Data record writes used preview and explicit apply confirmation.
4. Script artifact writes used `artifact_create` or `artifact_update`, not record CRUD.
5. Existing artifacts were fetched before modification.
6. Risky operations had explicit confirmation naming the target.
7. Created or updated records were fetched or reviewed after write when the MCP supports it.
8. Script artifacts were checked with `docs_review_notes` and `docs_test_scenarios`.
9. The resulting `sys_id`, record number, or URL was reported to the user.
10. Warnings, row limits, masked fields, and partial failures were surfaced.

<path>/Users/lasn/Projects/servicenow-development/agentrules/skills/snu-agent-api/SKILL.md</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: name: snu-agent-api
3: description: SN ScriptSync HTTP/file Agent API: endpoint discovery, auth, the full error-code table, and the complete command catalog (query_records, get_record, update_record, create_artifact, create_application, rest_request, screenshots, etc.). Read this before calling any Agent API command.
4: ---
5: 
6: <!-- SN-SCRIPTSYNC:SKILL instructionsSchemaVersion=23 -->
7: 
8: # SN ScriptSync — Agent API
9: 
10: Full reference for the SN ScriptSync Agent API and every command except the live-form g_form bridge (see the snu-form-automation skill) and the browser debugger (see the snu-browser-debug skill).
11: 
12: # SN ScriptSync - Agent API
13: 
14: The Agent API lets AI assistants drive the extension: sync files, create/update records, query ServiceNow, take screenshots, and more. Two transports are available. Prefer the HTTP transport; the file transport is kept for backward compatibility.
15: 
16: > **The browser helper tab is still required.** Both transports only change the *agent <-> extension* hop. Anything that touches ServiceNow (record reads/writes, queries, screenshots) still round-trips through the SN Utils helper tab over WebSocket. Keep a helper tab open (run `/token` in a browser session) or commands return `E_BROWSER_DISCONNECTED`.
17: 
18: ## Transport 1: HTTP (recommended, event-driven)
19: 
20: When the extension is running it spins up a local HTTP server bound to `127.0.0.1`, preferring the **fixed port 1977** (ephemeral fallback if taken). The actual port and auth token are published to two port files — `~/.sn-scriptsync/agent-port.json` (well-known, discoverable from any directory; written only while the connected browser has an active SN Utils Pro/Trial/Enterprise license) and `<workspace>/.vscode/sn-agent-port.json` (always written — workspace agent workflows are free):
21: 
22: ```json
23: {
24:   "port": 1977,
25:   "token": "4f9a...hex...",
26:   "pid": 68861,
27:   "apiVersion": 6,
28:   "startedAt": 1734000000000
29: }
30: ```
31: 
32: ### Discovering the endpoint
33: 
34: ```bash
35: # Unix / macOS — global file works from any cwd; older versions only write the workspace file
36: PORT=$(jq -r .port ~/.sn-scriptsync/agent-port.json 2>/dev/null || jq -r .port .vscode/sn-agent-port.json)
37: TOKEN=$(jq -r .token ~/.sn-scriptsync/agent-port.json 2>/dev/null || jq -r .token .vscode/sn-agent-port.json)
38: ```
39: 
40: ```powershell
41: # Windows PowerShell
42: $cfg = Get-Content "$HOME/.sn-scriptsync/agent-port.json" | ConvertFrom-Json
43: $PORT  = $cfg.port
44: $TOKEN = $cfg.token
45: ```
46: 
47: ### Required discovery algorithm (do this every session)
48: 
49: Port files can be **stale** (leftover from a crash; the workspace copy may even be synced
50: by iCloud/OneDrive/git from another machine), so never trust one blindly and never cache
51: the port/token. Each session:
52: 
53: 1. Read `port`, `token`, and `pid` from `~/.sn-scriptsync/agent-port.json`, falling back to
54:    `<workspace>/.vscode/sn-agent-port.json` (older versions write only the workspace file).
55: 2. Call `GET http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/health`.
56: 3. Trust the endpoint **only if** all hold:
57:    - the health request succeeds (HTTP 200), and
58:    - `health.pid` equals the `pid` from the port file (confirms the file matches the live server), and
59:    - `health.apiVersion` is one you support.
60: 4. If any check fails (no file, connection refused, pid mismatch, version mismatch),
61:    the HTTP server is not usable here — fall back to the file transport below.
62: 5. Detect available commands from `health.commands[]` rather than hard-coding the list;
63:    it changes between versions.
64: 
65: ### Health check (no auth)
66: 
67: ```bash
68: curl -s http://127.0.0.1:$PORT/api/health
69: # → { "status": "success", "apiVersion": 6, "commands": [...], "pid": 68861 }
70: ```
71: 
72: The extension deletes both port files when the server stops, but a crash or a sync
73: conflict can leave a stale file behind — which is exactly why the `pid` cross-check
74: in step 3 is mandatory before sending real commands.
75: 
76: ### Self-describing docs (no auth)
77: 
78: The running server serves its own documentation, so instructions can never drift from
79: the installed version: `GET /api/instructions` (connect guide + core instructions),
80: `GET /api/skills` (skill index), `GET /api/skills/<name>` (full skill markdown). If you
81: were handed only the endpoint — no workspace, no files — start there.
82: 
83: ### Sending a command
84: 
85: Every authenticated request is `POST /api` with a JSON body. Provide an `X-Agent-Token` header.
86: 
87: ```bash
88: curl -s -X POST http://127.0.0.1:$PORT/api \
89:   -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
90:   -H "X-Agent-Token: $TOKEN" \
91:   -d '{
92:         "command": "check_connection",
93:         "instance": "dev12345"
94:       }'
95: ```
96: 
97: Response:
98: 
99: ```json
100: {
101:   "id": "http_1734000001234_a1b2c3",
102:   "command": "check_connection",
103:   "status": "success",
104:   "result": { "ready": true, "appName": "Cursor" },
105:   "timestamp": 1734000001235
106: }
107: ```
108: 
109: If `id` is omitted, the server generates one. Errors return a JSON body with `status: "error"` and a structured `code`:
110: 
111: ```json
112: { "status": "error", "code": "E_BROWSER_DISCONNECTED", "error": "No browser helper tab is connected." }
113: ```
114: 
115: HTTP status codes map to codes:
116: 
117: | Code | HTTP | Meaning |
118: |------|------|---------|
119: | `E_INVALID_PARAMS` / `E_INVALID_REQUEST` / `E_CONFIRM_REQUIRED` | 400 | Malformed request, or a destructive command called without `confirm:true` |
120: | `E_UNAUTHORIZED` | 401 | Missing/invalid `X-Agent-Token` |
121: | `E_UNKNOWN_COMMAND` / `E_NOT_FOUND` | 404 | No such command, or the target record doesn't exist |
122: | `E_REFERENCE_INTEGRITY` | 409 | Write/delete blocked by a reference or data-integrity constraint |
123: | `E_INSTANCE_REQUIRED` / `E_INSTANCE_NOT_FOUND` | 422 | Instance not resolvable |
124: | `E_DISABLED` | 423 | Feature disabled via settings (e.g. deletes, background scripts) |
125: | `E_PAUSED` | 423 | The user paused agent access in the ScriptSync helper tab. Stop and tell the user — do not retry until they press Resume agents there |
126: | `E_PARTIAL_FAILURE` | 207 | Batch partially succeeded — inspect per-item results |
127: | `E_REVIEW_PENDING` | 202 | The command needs human approval in the helper tab Review Queue. **Tell the user to approve it** in the SN Utils ScriptSync helper tab in their browser, then call `get_review_result` with the `reviewId` from `details` to collect the outcome |
128: | `E_USER_REJECTED` | 403 | The developer rejected the command in the Review Queue — do not retry without asking the user |
129: | `E_COMMAND_FAILED` | 500 | The command was accepted (and, when applicable, approved) but failed during execution. Inspect `details.status`, `details.detail`, and `details.response`; this is not a user rejection |
130: | `E_INTERNAL` | 500 | Unexpected error |
131: | `E_ACL` / `E_TOKEN_EXPIRED` / `E_SCREENSHOT_PERMISSION` | 502 | ServiceNow rejected the request, or a tab needs a one-time capture grant (click the SN Utils icon on it, then retry) |
132: | `E_SERVER_NOT_RUNNING` / `E_BROWSER_DISCONNECTED` | 503 | Can't reach ServiceNow |
133: | `E_TIMEOUT` | 504 | Round-trip exceeded deadline |
134: 
135: ### Resolving `E_INSTANCE_REQUIRED` (multiple instances)
136: 
137: When a command returns `E_INSTANCE_REQUIRED`, you didn't pass `"instance"` and
138: the bridge could not select a single safe target. `details.knownInstances` lists
139: remembered workspace folders; `details.connectedInstances` lists the subset
140: observed on the current helper connection. A folder is not proof of a live
141: session. `auth_status` automatically selects the target only when exactly one
142: known folder matches a helper-observed instance.
143: 
144: If the error says `Multiple known workspace instances found`, do not describe
145: those folders as active connections. If it says `Multiple helper-connected
146: instances found`, ask for a target (especially for a write) or use the roster
147: and freshness guidance below.
148: 
149: Use freshness as a *default pick*, not an exclusivity test. The extension
150: rewrites `<instance>/_settings.json` (refreshing `g_ck`) every time it relays for
151: that instance, so the **most recently modified `_settings.json` is the
152: most-recently-active session**.
153: 
154: The fastest path — and the one to prefer — is the one-shot **`list_instances`**
155: command. It's purely local (no browser round-trip, never returns
156: `E_INSTANCE_REQUIRED`) and returns the whole roster, each `url`, a freshest-first
157: ranking, and a suggested `defaultInstance`:
158: 
159: 1. Call `list_instances`. If `defaultInstance` is set (exactly one instance was
160:    recently active), retry your command with `"instance": defaultInstance`.
161: 2. If `needsConfirmation` is `true` (none recent, or two-plus recent — e.g.
162:    `ven08329` + `ven08331`), or the operation is a write / destructive action,
163:    confirm the target with the user instead of guessing.
164: 3. Optionally confirm the chosen instance's bridge actually responds with a cheap
165:    `get_instance_info` (check `recentlyActive` / `lastActiveAgeMs`) or
166:    `query_records` round-trip before committing — `connected` is bridge-level and
167:    is `true` for every instance whenever the helper tab is up, so it doesn't
168:    disambiguate.
169: 
170: If `list_instances` isn't available (older extension), fall back to reading the
171: `<instance>/_settings.json` mtimes yourself and applying the same rule. Once
172: resolved, reuse that `instance` for the rest of the session.
173: 
174: ## Transport: HTTP only
175: 
176: The legacy file-based transport (`{instance_folder}/agent/requests/*.json`) was removed in 4.8.0. Every agent flow uses the HTTP Agent API above; if a request file is dropped in the workspace nothing will pick it up.
177: 
178: ## Commands
179: 
180: ### `check_connection` ⚡ (CALL THIS FIRST)
181: Verify WebSocket server is running and browser helper tab is connected. **Always call this before any other operations.**
182: 
183: **Request:**
184: ```json
185: { "id": "0", "command": "check_connection" }
186: ```
187: 
188: **Response (ready):**
189: ```json
190: {
191:   "status": "success",
192:   "result": {
193:     "ready": true,
194:     "serverRunning": true,
195:     "browserConnected": true,
196:     "message": "Connected and ready",
197:     "helper": { "debuggerAvailable": false, "tier": "pro", "proFeatures": true }
198:   }
199: }
200: ```
201: 
202: **`helper` tells you which SN Utils build is connected.** `debuggerAvailable` is true only on the SN Utils Debug edition; most users run the regular build. You don't need this to take screenshots (`take_screenshot` auto-routes to the best available path), but it tells you what else this session can do:
203: 
204: - `debuggerAvailable: false` → explicit debugger commands (`capture_full_page`, network/console capture, dialog handling) will return `E_CDP_UNAVAILABLE`, and a screenshot on an ungranted tab will require the user's one-time icon click.
205: - `debuggerAvailable: true` + `proFeatures: true` → full-page/element captures and network/console/dialog debugging are available (subject to the `browserDebugger` gate — see `get_capabilities`), and screenshots never need a permission click.
206: - `helper: null` → the handshake hasn't arrived yet (or the license lookup failed); retry or fall back to `get_capabilities` for the authoritative, browser-verified view.
207: 
208: **Response (server not running):**
209: ```json
210: {
211:   "status": "error",
212:   "error": "WebSocket server not running. Click sn-scriptsync in VS Code status bar to start.",
213:   "result": {
214:     "ready": false,
215:     "serverRunning": false,
216:     "browserConnected": false,
217:     "message": "WebSocket server not running"
218:   }
219: }
220: ```
221: 
222: **Response (no browser):**
223: ```json
224: {
225:   "status": "error",
226:   "error": "No browser connection. Open SN Utils helper tab via /token command in ServiceNow.",
227:   "result": {
228:     "ready": false,
229:     "serverRunning": true,
230:     "browserConnected": false,
231:     "message": "No browser connected - open helper tab with /token"
232:   }
233: }
234: ```
235: 
236: ### `get_capabilities` ⚡ (preflight Pro / debugger / security gates)
237: Ask the connected SN Utils helper tab what it can do **right now** — the Agent API version, the license tier, whether the Chrome DevTools Protocol **browser debugger** (network/console capture, full-page screenshots, native dialog handling) is usable, which protocol **`capabilities`** the helper supports (two-phase command review, per-instance security gates), and the per-instance **`instanceGates`** snapshots. Call this once up front so you can preflight `E_DISABLED` instead of discovering it mid-operation, and before reaching for the `snu-browser-debug` skill instead of firing a CDP command and parsing the error.
238: 
239: Requires a connected helper tab (`E_BROWSER_DISCONNECTED` otherwise — run `check_connection` first).
240: 
241: **Request:**
242: ```json
243: { "id": "cap_1", "command": "get_capabilities" }
244: ```
245: 
246: **Response (v8 helper with per-instance gates):**
247: ```json
248: {
249:   "status": "success",
250:   "result": {

(Showing lines 1-250 of 2164. Use offset=251 to continue.)
</content>
```

## Assistant (Servicenow · GPT-6 Astra · 24.1s)

_Thinking:_



Der er nu to konkrete fund:

- Task-kontrollen kører ved order `200`, mens `task closer` først kan sætte RITM-state til `3` ved order `900`. Det giver en mulig vej uden om kontrollen, når lukningen starter med `active=false`.
- `active` og `state` er ikke altid ensrettet. Jeg fandt to RITM-records med state `-5` og `active=false`. Audit på den ene viser først lukning til `3`, derefter skift tilbage til `-5`, uden at `active` blev sat til true.

Jeg fandt også jobbet `Luk tasks`. Trods navnet sletter det tasks; det lukker dem ikke. Jobbet er sat til on-demand og er ikke kørt under undersøgelsen.

**Tool: servicenow_flow**

  "sys_id": "130220ea879f6a108643202cbbbb35d1",

  "section_limit": 30
}

## Assistant (Servicenow · GPT-6 Astra · 116.9s)

_Thinking:_



# Teknisk kortlægning: lukning af sc_task og sc_req_item

**Grundlag:** Read-only undersøgelse af `tv2dev`, Global, den 7. september 2026. Ingen konfigurationer eller data er ændret.

**Status:** De centrale lukningsmekanismer og konkrete huller i valideringen er identificeret. Kortlægningen er **ikke fuldt dækkende for alle scripts og Flow/Workflow-grene**. Begrænsninger og næste undersøgelser fremgår nedenfor.

## 1. State-værdier og active

Tabellerne arver `state` og `active` fra `task`. Der blev ikke fundet dictionary overrides for disse felter på `sc_task` eller `sc_req_item`.

| Tabel | Åbne state-værdier med aktive choices | Lukkede state-værdier |
|---|---|---|
| `sc_task` | `-5` Pending, `1` Open, `2` Work in Progress | `3` Closed Complete, `7` Closed Skipped. Desuden behandler serverlogikken `4` som lukket. |
| `sc_req_item` | `-5` Pending, `1` New, `2` Work in Progress, `8` Assigned, `9` Awaiting approval, `40` Awaiting vendor, `50` Updated by vendor, `60` Updated by user, `61` Awaiting User Info | `3` Closed Complete, `7` Closed Skipped. Desuden behandler serverlogikken `4` som lukket. |

**Vigtig uoverensstemmelse:** RITM-choice `4 = Closed Incomplete` er inaktiv. På `task` findes en inaktiv choice `4 = Awaiting User Info`. Samtidig bruger `TaskStateUtil` som standard **`3,4,7` som lukkede states**.

Et skjult eller inaktivt choice forhindrer derfor ikke, at scripts bruger værdien. Brug ikke numeriske intervaller som `state > 2` til at afgøre, om en RITM er lukket.

### Stemmer active med state?

**Ikke altid.** Blandt RITM-records opdateret i de seneste 90 dage fandtes:

- 30 aktive RITM-records i åbne states.
- 11 inaktive RITM-records i state `3` eller `7`.
- **2 inaktive RITM-records i state `-5`:** `RITM0184918` og `RITM0168626`.

Audit på `RITM0184918` viser den 23. juni 2026:

1. State ændres fra `2` til `3`, stage til `complete` og active til false.
2. Næste record-version ændrer state fra `3` til `-5`.
3. Active forbliver false.

Det dokumenterer inkonsistensen, men ikke alene den præcise udførende kode. Der blev ikke fundet åbne tasks under state-lukkede RITM-records i det anvendte 90-dages task-udsnit. Det udelukker ikke ældre tilfælde.

## 2. Centrale Business Rules og Script Includes

Alle nedenstående Business Rules er aktive.

| Mekanisme | Timing/order | Betydning |
|---|---|---|
| [`RITM_Check_Open_Tasks`](https://tv2dev.service-now.com/sys_script.do?sys_id=37e70725db10001073178e47489619c7) | RITM, before insert/update, **200** | Blokerer skift til `3` eller `7`, hvis en `sc_task` med `request_item=current.sys_id` har `active=true`. Har tekstbaserede undtagelser. |
| `Task Active State Management` | Task, before, **50** | Bruger `TaskStateUtil` ved state-ændringer. Den fulde kode kunne ikke gennemgås pålideligt gennem værktøjets komprimerede svar. |
| `Set Active Flag` | RITM, before, **100** | Sætter active=false ved terminal stage. Kan også sætte state `4`/`7` ved delivery plans og annullere workflow. |
| `mark closed` | Task, before, **800** | Sætter active=false, når `TaskStateUtil.runMarkClosed()` tillader det. |
| [`task closer`](https://tv2dev.service-now.com/sys_script.do?sys_id=475ef3c5c611228401440a7a5f29a786) | Task, before, **900** | Sætter state til standardlukket state, normalt `3`, ved deaktivering af en åben task-record. |
| `task reopener` | Task, before, **901** | Sætter state til standardåben state og active=true, når dens betingelse opfyldes. |
| `Set Active Flag After Workflows` | RITM, before, **10100** | Gentager terminal-stage-behandlingen sent i transaktionen. |
| `Close Ticket` | sc_task, before, **100** | Behandler skift til `3`, `4` eller `7`; indeholder active- og afslutningstidslogik. |
| `Close Tasks Due to Cancellations` | RITM, after, **100** | Ved stage `Request Cancelled` sætter den tasks fundet via **parent** til state `4`. |
| [`Close Tasks when Closed Skipped`](https://tv2dev.service-now.com/sys_script.do?sys_id=c5b03a0d1baf3f4071f121b4bd4bcb43) | RITM, before, **100** | Behandler de tekstbaserede undtagelser ved state `7`. Sætter tasks fundet via **parent** til `7`. Har også REQ-opdatering med `setWorkflow(false)`. |

Andre relevante afhængigheder:

- **Delivery plans:** `Auto Close on Approval`, `Process Rejection`, `Reject Parent` og `Start Peer Tasks`.
- **Workflow-genoptagelse:** `SNC - Run parent workflows`.
- **REQ-kaskader:** `request closure`, `request item closure`, `Close Parent if Required`, `RITM_On_Close_Check_Other_RITM` og `REQ_Check_Open_RITM`.
- **Afslutningsfelter:** `Set Closure Fields`.
- **Script Includes:** `TaskStateUtil`, `CatalogRequestUtil`, `CatalogTransactionLineItemVeto` samt delivery-plan- og Item Designer-hjælpere.

`WorkflowStages` og Client Script `ProcesFlow` må ikke forveksles med lukningskontroller. `ProcesFlow` ændrer bredden på procesformatterens visning.

## 3. Identificerede lukningsveje

| Vej | Vurdering |
|---|---|
| **UI: RITM-state og Update** | Mulig for brugere med de nødvendige skriverettigheder. Den aktive RITM-UI Action `Update` kalder `current.update()`. Servervalideringen gælder, når dens betingelser er opfyldt. |
| **UI: Close Task** | Flere aktive UI Actions sætter `sc_task.state=3` og gemmer. Workspace-varianten kræver særskilt kontrol af klientdelen. |
| **UI: TV 2 CleanUp Routine** | Sætter RITM til `7` og indsætter præcis den kommentar, der undtager task-kontrollen. |
| **UI: Create Incident** | Aktiv handling for `itil`. Scriptet indeholder den tekst, som task-kontrollen undtager. Hele konverteringsforløbet er ikke verificeret. |
| **Flow Designer** | Bekræftet: eksempelvis OneTrust og GitHub har Update Record-trin til RITM-state `3`/`7`. AWS-flowet opretter en task med Wait aktiveret. Vent på én task er ikke en samlet kontrol af alle tilknyttede tasks. |
| **Legacy Workflow** | Bekræftet: Set Values-aktiviteter sætter RITM-state og terminal stage. Eksempelvis sætter aktiviteten “Close task” i `SD - ASsign. Grp. Ode - M/ approvel` **RITM-state=3**, ikke sc_task-state. |
| **REST/Table API** | En mulig skrivevej under ACL-kontrol. Normale opdateringer rammer serverreglerne; UI-regler beskytter ikke denne vej. Ingen skriveforsøg er udført. |
| **Server-side scripts** | Bekræftet via Business Rules, UI Actions og workflow-kode. Scripts kan skrive state, active eller stage. Privilegeret kode, som frakobler Business Rules, kan omgå en Business Rule-validering. |
| **Jobbet “Luk tasks”** | **Sletter**, frem for at lukke, sc_task-records i state `1`, når deres RITM er i state `7`. Aktivt, men `run_type=on_demand`. Må ikke bruges som oprydning uden særskilt godkendelse. |

Der blev fundet **65 publicerede RITM-workflowversioner**, to REQ-workflowversioner og otte katalogitems med Flow Designer-reference. Alle grene er ikke gennemgået.

### Client Scripts, UI Policies og ACL’er

- Den undersøgte Global-liste indeholder ingen aktiv onSubmit-kontrol af åbne RITM-tasks.
- `Check Parent Request Closed` er et aktivt onLoad-script på sc_task. Fuld adfærd er fortsat uafklaret.
- De fundne UI Policy Actions ændrer ikke state eller active og håndhæver ikke child-task-kontrollen.
- ACL’er giver blandt andet tabeladgang via `itil`/`sn_request_write`. `task.state` har rollerne `itil`/`task_editor`.
- De gennemgåede relevante write-ACL’er kontrollerer **ikke åbne child tasks**. Tabeladgang alene er ikke lig med adgang til alle felter.
- Ingen direkte Global-transform maps til de undersøgte tabeller blev fundet. Ingen direkte tabelreferencer blev fundet i Global Scripted REST-operationer. Dynamiske og indirekte kald er ikke dermed udelukket.

## 4. Hvorfor kan en RITM lukkes med åbne tasks?

Der findes en serverkontrol, men den dækker ikke alle lukningsformer:

1. **Kontrollen kører for tidligt.**  
   Ved order `200` undersøges kun state-skift til `3`/`7`. En opdatering med active=false kan først blive omsat til state `3` af `task closer` ved order `900`. Det er et konkret hul i regelrækkefølgen, som skal reproduceres i en kontrolleret test.

2. **State `4` er ikke dækket.**  
   Den behandles som lukket af serverlogikken, men indgår ikke i `RITM_Check_Open_Tasks`.

3. **Undtagelser styres af journaltekst.**  
   En bestemt inaktivitetskommentar eller teksten `Closed requested item and created incident` springer kontrollen over.

4. **“Åben task” betyder kun active=true i kontrollen.**  
   En task med åben state og active=false overses.

5. **Relationerne er forskellige.**  
   Kontrollen bruger `request_item`. Kaskadelukningen bruger `parent`. Uens relationer kan derfor give forskellige task-sæt.

6. **Der ses ingen kontrol mod senere åbne tasks i de gennemgåede regler.**  
   En lukket RITM kan potentielt få en ny, genåbnet eller omtilknyttet task.

**Konklusion:** Konfigurationshullerne er dokumenteret. Den historiske årsag til det oprindelige tilfælde er **uafklaret**, fordi den konkrete record ikke længere foreligger.

## 5. Anbefaling til EUC-2153

**Udvid eller erstat den eksisterende kontrol med én fælles servervalidering. Undgå endnu en parallel regel med delvist overlappende betingelser.**

Valideringen bør:

- Bruge `request_item` som autoritativ task-relation.
- Dække state `3,4,7`, deaktivering og terminal-stage-lukning.
- Kontrollere den endelige lukkestatus efter relevante state-/stage-ændringer. Et order-skift alene er ikke tilstrækkeligt.
- Under oprydningen blokere ved **åben state eller active=true** på en tilknyttet task. Inkonsistente tasks skal rettes, ikke overses.
- Bruge et begrænset eksistensopslag frem for at hente og tælle alle tasks.
- Afbryde gemningen med en tydelig fejl. API og Flow skal også håndtere afvisningen.
- Fjerne journaltekst som autorisation til at springe valideringen over.
- Suppleres med kontrol ved oprettelse, genåbning og omtilknytning af tasks til en lukket RITM.

**Annullering og konvertering:** Afslut eller flyt relevante tasks først. Verificér resultatet. Luk derefter RITM. Gennemgå de eksisterende before-reglers sideeffekter, før en sen validering indføres.

## 6. Undtagelser, risici og åbne beslutninger

Der er implementeret særforløb for inaktivitet, konvertering til incident og annullering. **Der er ikke dokumenteret en godkendt forretningsundtagelse, som tillader, at tasks permanent forbliver åbne under en lukket RITM.**

Følgende skal afklares før implementering:

- Skal state `4` fortsat understøttes, og hvilke labels skal gælde?
- Skal alle åbne tasks blokere, eller findes godkendte efterbehandlingsopgaver?
- Hvordan skal annullering påvirke tasks, approvals og kørende workflows?
- Hvordan håndteres samtidig RITM-lukning og task-oprettelse?
- Hvilke SLA’er, notifikationer, REQ-kaskader og integrationer reagerer på lukning?

**Værktøjs- og dækningsbegrænsninger:**

- Flere scripts blev returneret komprimeret og er ikke fuldt gennemgået.
- Flow-opslag fejlede for DX, Atlassian og Convert Service Offering to Business Application.
- AWS, GitHub, headset og DX har forskellige master/latest snapshots. Den udførte version skal verificeres.
- Workflow-aktivitetslisten var begrænset til 100 af 238 kandidater.
- Scoped apps er uden for den aftalte Global-afgrænsning.

**Acceptkriteriet “alle relevante mekanismer” er derfor ikke endeligt opfyldt.** Næste undersøgelse bør færdiggøre disse afgrænsede punkter og teste UI, API og Flow med åbne tasks, state `4`, active-only-lukning, terminal stage, annullering og genåbning. Det kan ske på kontrollerede testrecords i `tv2dev`.

Er der noget som du ikke har adgang til siden du ikke er mega konkret?

## Assistant (Servicenow · GPT-6 Astra · 22.7s)

_Thinking:_



Ja, der var begrænsninger i værktøjernes svar. **Men jeg har ikke dokumentation for, at jeg mangler adgangsrettigheder til de relevante konfigurationer.** Jeg skulle have skelnet tydeligere mellem de to ting.

### Det konkrete problem

- **Flere scripts blev erstattet af komprimeringsmarkører** som `<<ccr:...>>`. Jeg kunne læse konfigurationen, men ikke altid hele scriptet. Gentagne opslag løste det kun for nogle scripts.
- **Flow-værktøjet fejlede** ved opslag på DX, Atlassian og Convert Service Offering to Business Application. Det var parsingfejl, ikke en besked om manglende adgang.
- **Flow-søgningen returnerede uvedkommende flows.** Den kunne derfor ikke bruges som bevis for fuld dækning.
- **Jeg gennemgik ikke alle sider af workflow-resultaterne.** Det er uafsluttet arbejde fra min side, ikke en adgangsbegrænsning.
- **Jeg udførte ingen lukningstest**, fordi opgaven var read-only. Derfor er hullet mellem order `200` og `900` en kodebaseret forklaring, ikke en reproduceret fejl.

### Det ved jeg med sikkerhed

Den eksisterende `RITM_Check_Open_Tasks`:
- kører før insert/update med order **200**;
- kontrollerer kun skift til **3 eller 7**;
- søger tasks via **request_item og active=true**;
- har **tekstbaserede undtagelser**;
- afbryder gemningen, hvis den finder en matchende task.

Desuden er **state/active-inkonsistens dokumenteret på konkrete records**, og jobbet **“Luk tasks” sletter faktisk records**.

### Hvorfor svaret ikke var skarpere

Jeg stoppede kortlægningen for tidligt og blandede verificerede fund, mulige forklaringer og uafsluttede undersøgelser. Det er mit ansvar. **Du skal ikke give mig flere rettigheder på det foreliggende grundlag.**

Det næste er at færdiggøre de målrettede read-only opslag og hente de manglende scriptdefinitioner gennem en fungerende læsevej. Først hvis der kommer en konkret adgangsafvisning, kan jeg sige præcist, hvilken adgang der mangler.
